Why use OneHotEncoder on Iris?¶

  • Iris dataset labels: 0, 1, 2 for three classes.
  • Some ML models (especially NN with softmax + categorical_crossentropy) require one-hot labels for correct training.

One-hot encoding converts:

  • 0 → [1, 0, 0]
  • 1 → [0, 1, 0]
  • 2 → [0, 0, 1]

Implementation with sklearn¶

from sklearn.preprocessing import OneHotEncoder
from sklearn.datasets import load_iris

iris = load_iris()
y = iris.target.reshape(-1, 1)  # required shape

encoder = OneHotEncoder(sparse=False)
y_encoded = encoder.fit_transform(y)

print(y_encoded[:5])  # Check first few encoded labels

Softmax: A Clean Practical Intro¶

A structured Colab-ready note on what softmax is, how it works, and when to use it in ML/DL.

What is Softmax?¶

Softmax is an activation function that:

  • Converts raw scores (logits) into probabilities.
  • Ensures all output values are between 0 and 1.
  • Ensures all probabilities sum to 1.

The Formula¶

Given logits: $$ z = [z_1, z_2, ..., z_n] $$

Softmax for class ( j ) is: $$ \sigma(z)_j = \frac{e^{z_j}}{\sum_{k=1}^{n} e^{z_k}} $$

Why exponential?¶

  • Emphasizes larger logits (higher confidence).
  • Stabilizes probability distribution.

When to use Softmax?¶

Use softmax in:

  • Multi-class classification problems where:
    • Each sample belongs to exactly one class.
    • Classes are mutually exclusive.
  • As the activation function in the last layer of your neural network when you want probability outputs across multiple classes.

Typically paired with:

  • Loss: categorical_crossentropy (one-hot labels).
  • OR sparse_categorical_crossentropy (integer labels).

When NOT to use softmax:¶

  • Binary classification → Use sigmoid instead.
  • Multi-label classification (sample can belong to multiple classes simultaneously) → Use multiple sigmoid outputs instead.

Example in Keras¶

Model:¶

from tensorflow.keras import Sequential
from tensorflow.keras.layers import Dense

model = Sequential([
    Dense(32, activation='relu', input_shape=(4,)),
    Dense(3, activation='softmax')
])

In [ ]:
model.compile(loss='categorical_crossentropy',optimizer=optimizer,metrics=['accuracy'])

Why use loss='categorical_crossentropy'¶

Use when:

  • Multi-class, single-label classification.
  • Labels are one-hot encoded.
  • Model output uses softmax.

Why:

  • Measures how well predicted probabilities match true labels.
  • Penalizes wrong confident predictions.
  • Perfectly matches softmax outputs, expecting probabilities that sum to 1.

Formula: $$ L = - \sum_{i} y_i \log(\hat{y}_i) $$ where:

  • $ y_i $: one-hot true label.
  • $ \hat{y}_i $: softmax output.

Alternative: Use sparse_categorical_crossentropy if using integer labels (0,1,2) instead of one-hot.

from sklearn.metrics import confusion_matrix, ConfusionMatrixDisplay, accuracy_score

cm = confusion_matrix(true_classes, predicted_classes)
disp = ConfusionMatrixDisplay(confusion_matrix=cm, display_labels=iris_data.target_names)

disp.plot(cmap='Blues', values_format='d')
